H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}\equiv H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
.
Suppose now that
\mu>0
. Denote
h(x)
the right hand side of (
3.6
). For all
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
D(x)<\frac{\mu}{a}
and any sequence
(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
x_{n}\to\ x
in
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
we see that
h(x_{n})=-\frac{\mu^{2}}{a}=h(x)
for sufficiently large
n
, and thus
\liminf_{n\to\infty}h(x_{n})=h(x)
. Fix
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
D(x)>\frac{\mu}{a}
. The function
f\colon[\frac{\mu}{a},\infty)\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{\pm\infty\},z\mapsto f(z)=-\mu z+\frac{a}{2}z^{2}
is continuous and increasing, and
h=f\circ D
. The particle density,
D\colon\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\to[0,\infty],x\mapsto\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}
, is lower semicontinuous. For all
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
D(x)=\infty
, we get
h(x)=\infty
and for any
(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
x_{n}\to x
as
n\to\infty
we see that
h(x_{n})\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. Suppose now that
x_{0}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
D(x_{0})\in(\frac{\mu}{a},\infty)
. For any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
such that
\lvert f(D(x_{0})-\widetilde{\varepsilon})-f(D(x_{0}))\rvert<\varepsilon
using the continuity of
f
. As
D
is lower semicontinuous, there exists a neighbourhood
{\mathcal{U}}(x_{x})
such that

D(x)\geq D(x_{0})-\widetilde{\varepsilon}\quad\mbox{ for all }x\in{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0}).

Using the fact that
f
is increasing, we obtain

\displaystyle f(D(x))\geq f(D(x_{0})-\widetilde{\varepsilon})\geq f(D(x_{0}))-\widetilde{\varepsilon}\quad\mbox{ for all }\in{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0}),

and thus proving that
h
is lower semicontinuous. We are left to show that
h
is the largest lower semicontinuous function smaller or equal to
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
. It suffices to consider only
x^{0}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
D(x^{0})<\frac{\mu}{a}
as in the other cases
h
coincides with
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
. Define
x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
by

x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}=x^{0}_{k}+\frac{1}{n}\big(\frac{\mu}{a}-D(x^{0})\big){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{n=k\},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
D(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})=\frac{\mu}{a}
, and thus

-\frac{\mu^{2}}{2a}=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}(x^{0})\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})=-\frac{\mu^{2}}{2a}.

The greatest lower semicontinuous function that satisfies this is the right hand side in (
3.6
).
∎

Proposition 3.8

(
Upper bound PMF-model
)
.

For all
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\alpha\leq 0
, and
a>0
,

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\Big]\leq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\Big\{I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(x)\Big\}.

(3.7)

Proof.
The statement follows easily with the upper bound estimate in Varadhan’s Lemma given in
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.6]
using the inequality
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)\geq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}(x)
for all
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, the lower semicontinuity of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}
, and the fact that
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}(x)\geq-\frac{\mu^{2}}{2a}
. The later estimate provides the tail-condition necessary to apply
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.6]
.
∎

Proposition 3.9

(
Lower bound PMF-model
)
.

For all
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\alpha\leq 0
, and
a>0
,

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\Big]\geq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\Big\{I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(x)\Big\}.

(3.8)

Proof.
The strategy for proving the lower bound is to first introduce a cut-off parameter as done in
[
ACK11
]
, that is, we change the measure to obtain a finite-dimensional problem which gives continuity of the Hamiltonian and thus a large deviation lower bound for the finite-dimensional space. The final step is then to remove the cut-off parameter. As our Hamiltonian is not positive, removing of the cut-off is not as straightforward as in
[
ACK11
]
. We thus need to construct a sequence for the finite dimensional spaces which allows for energy estimates and at the same time gives convergence towards the lower semicontinuous regularisation. The last step is crucial as a lower bound can be obtained via the upper semicontinuous regularisation which in our case is identical to infinity.

Step 1: Restriction of the mark space.
We will approximate the mark space
E
by the cut-off version

E^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}{\mathcal{C}}_{k},\quad K\in\mathbb{N}.

The Poisson reference process on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times E^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}
is denoted by
{\tt Q}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}
, and the corresponding measure on
\mathbb{R}^{K}
which is isomorphic to
\pi_{K}(\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}))
with
\pi_{K}\colon\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}^{k},x\mapsto(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K})
, is denoted
\nu_{N,\alpha}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}={\tt Q}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}\circ\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\Lambda_{N}}^{-1}=\nu_{N,\alpha}\circ\pi_{K}^{-1}
. We obtain a large deviation principle for the cut-off version in the following.

Lemma 3.10
.

For given
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\leq 0
, the sequence
(\nu_{N,\alpha}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies an LDP on
\mathbb{R}^{K}
with rate
\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert
and rate function

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x)=\begin{cases}\sum^{K}_{k=1}\big(x_{k}\log\frac{x_{k}}{q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}_{k}}-x_{k}+q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}_{k}\big),&x\in\mathbb{R}^{K}_{+},\\
+\infty,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(3.9)

Proof.
Since the projection
\pi_{K}
is continuous, we can apply the contraction principle to obtain a variational form of the rate function

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x)=\inf_{\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})\colon\pi_{K}(\widetilde{x})=x}I_{\alpha}(\widetilde{x}),

where
I_{\alpha}
is the rate function for
\nu_{N,\alpha}
in Proposition
1.2
.

If
x\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\setminus\mathbb{R}^{K}_{+}
, then any element
\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
with
\pi_{K}(\widetilde{x})=x
is such that
\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})\setminus\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, and hence
I(\widetilde{x})=+\infty
.

Now suppose
x\in\mathbb{R}^{K}_{+}
. Then if a corresponding
\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
was in fact in
\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})\setminus\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, then
I(\widetilde{x})=+\infty
. However, for all
\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
we have
I(\widetilde{x})<+\infty
. Hence we only need consider
\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
with positive entries. For
\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
\pi_{K}(\widetilde{x})=x\in\mathbb{R}^{K}_{+}
,

I_{\alpha}(\widetilde{x})=\sum^{K}_{k=1}x_{k}\Big(\log\frac{x_{k}}{q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}_{k}}-1\Big)+\sum^{\infty}_{k=K+1}\widetilde{x}_{k}\Big(\log\frac{\widetilde{x}_{k}}{q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}_{k}}-1\Big)+\overline{q}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}.

Thus is suffices to minimise the second term, which can be done term-wise. The infimum is given by
\widetilde{x}=y
, where

y_{k}=\begin{cases}x_{j},&k=1,\ldots,K\\
q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}_{k},&k>K.\end{cases}

This gives us

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x)=I_{\alpha}(\widetilde{x})=\sum^{K}_{k=1}x_{k}\Big(\log\frac{x_{k}}{q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}_{k}}-1\Big)-\sum^{\infty}_{j=K+1}q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}_{k}+\overline{q}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}},

exactly as required.
∎

Step 2: Lower bound.

We obtain inserting an indicator the lower bound,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\Big]\geq\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\nu_{N,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{K})=1\}\Big],

where we identified
\mathbb{R}^{K}
with the corresponding subspace in
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
. On that event we can replace
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
by
H_{\mu,K}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}
, where

H_{\mu,K}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}(x)=-\mu\sum_{k=1}^{K}kx_{k}+\frac{a}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}kx_{k}\Big)^{2},

and
\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}
by
\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}}
. The finite-dimensional approximation
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,K}
is in fact continuous, and thus we obtain a large deviation lower bound using Lemma
3.10
and Varadhan’s Lemma, see
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.4]
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\Big]\geq-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{K}}\big\{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,K}(x)\big\}

(3.10)

Step 3: Removing the cut-off parameter.
We are left to remove the cut-off by taking
K\to\infty
and to prove that the
K\to\infty
limit of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,K}
is replaced by the lower semicontinuous regularisation of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
.

Lemma 3.11
.

\limsup_{K\to\infty}\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{K}}\big\{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,K}(x)\big\}\leq\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(x)\big\}.

(3.11)

Proof.
Fix
\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
satisfying
I(\widetilde{x})+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha}(\widetilde{x})<\infty
. For
K\in\mathbb{N}
, consider
x^{K}=\pi_{K}(\widetilde{x})
. By (
3.9
), we have
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}(x^{K})\leq I(\widetilde{x})
. We shall replace
x^{K}
by
\widehat{x}^{K}
, defined as

\widehat{x}^{K}_{k}=\begin{cases}x^{K}_{k},&,k=1,\ldots,K-1,\\
x^{K}_{K}+\frac{1}{K}\big(\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}-D(\widetilde{x})\big)_{+},&,k=K.\end{cases}

Clearly,
\lVert x^{K}-\widehat{x}^{K}\rVert_{\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\to 0
as
K\to\infty
, and
x^{K}\to\widetilde{x}
as
K\to\infty
. Furthermore,

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}(\widehat{x}^{K})\leq I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}(x^{K})+\lvert I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}(\widehat{x}^{K})-I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}(x^{K})\rvert\leq I(\widetilde{x})+\text{O}(\frac{1}{K}\log K).

We turn to the energy term which needs extra care as the Hamiltonian is not positive. Observe that

D(\widehat{x}^{K})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}k\widehat{x}^{K}_{k}=\begin{cases}\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}+D(x^{K})-D(\widetilde{x})&,D(\widetilde{x})<\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a},\\
D(x^{K})&,D(\widetilde{x})\geq\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}.\end{cases}

Assume that
D(\widetilde{x})\geq\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}
. Then,

H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,K}(\widehat{x}^{K})=-\left(\mu+\alpha\right)\sum_{k=1}^{K}kx^{K}_{k}+\frac{a}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}kx^{K}_{k}\Big)^{2}\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(\widetilde{x}).

In the other case,
D(\widetilde{x})<\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}
, for every
\varepsilon>0
choose
K
sufficiently large such that
\lvert D(x^{K})-D(\widetilde{x})\rvert<\varepsilon
, and estimate

\displaystyle H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,K}(\widehat{x}^{K})

\displaystyle=-\mu\big(\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}+D(x^{K})-D(\widetilde{x})\big)+\frac{a}{2}\big(\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}+\big(D(x^{K})-D(\widetilde{x})\big)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(\widetilde{x})+2(\mu+\alpha)\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2}\frac{a}{2}

to finish the proof for (
3.11
).
∎

We finally combine Proposition
3.7
and Proposition
3.9
to finish the proof for Theorem
1.4
.

∎

3.4.
Proof of Theorem
1.6

This section proves Theorem
1.6
using techniques which are similar to the ones in the proof in Section
3.3
. However, there are significant differences to address due to the fact that the Hamiltonian
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
has positive and negative contributions. We rewrite the Hamiltonian in two equivalent ways for any
a\geq b>0
and
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=-\mu\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}+\frac{(a-b)}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}\Big)^{2}+\frac{b}{2}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j,k=1}{j\not= k}}^{\infty}jkx_{j}x_{k}

(3.12)

\displaystyle=-\mu\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}+\frac{a}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}\Big)^{2}-\frac{b}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}.

(3.13)

Note that that the right hand side in (
3.12
) is the sum of a PMF Hamiltonian with interaction strength
(a-b)
and a lower semicontinuous and non-negative term. On the other hand, (
3.13
) expresses
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}
as the sum of a PMF Hamiltonian, and an upper semicontinuous and non-positive term. Let us introduce the following notations

\displaystyle H_{\mu,a}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}(x)=-\mu\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}+\frac{a}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}\Big)^{2},\quad a>0,

\displaystyle H_{+}(x)=\frac{b}{2}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j,k=1}{j\not= k}}^{\infty}jkx_{j}x_{k},\qquad H_{-}(x)=-\frac{b}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}.

Thus
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}=H_{\mu,a}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}+H_{-}=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a-b}+H_{+}
.

Lemma 3.12
.

For
b>0
,
H_{-}
is upper semicontinuous and
H_{+}
is lower semicontinuous on
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.

Proof.
We shall show that
\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j,k=1}{j\not= k}}^{\infty}jkx_{j}x_{k}
and
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}
are both lower semicontinuous on
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Suppose
x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}\to x
in
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Clearly,

\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert\leq\lVert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}-x\rVert_{\ell_{1}}\quad\mbox{ for all }k\in\mathbb{N}.

Furthermore, due to the
\ell_{1}
-convergence and
\ell_{1}\subset\ell_{\infty}
, the term
\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert
is bounded in both
k
and
n
. Hence

\lvert(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k})^{2}-x_{k}^{2}\rvert=\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}+x_{k}\rvert\to 0\;\mbox{ as }n\to\infty.

Applying Fatou’s Lemma here proves that
H_{-}
is upper semicontinuous. Similarly, for all
(j,k)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
,

\lvert x_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}x_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}-x_{j}x_{k}\rvert\leq\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{j}\rvert\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert+\lvert x_{k}\rvert\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{j}-x_{j}\rvert\to 0\;\mbox{ as }n\to\infty.

This convergence in conjunction with Fatou’s Lemma shows that
H_{+}
is lower semicontinuous.
∎

Lemma 3.13
.

For
a>b
, the
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
lower semicontinuous regularisation of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
is given by

H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}(x)=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)-\frac{\big(\mu-aD(x)\big)_{+}^{2}}{2(a-b)},\quad x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}).

(3.14)

Proof.
Denote the right hand side of (
3.14
) by
h
. Clearly,
h(x)\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)
and
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,(a-b),l.s.c.}(x)\leq h(x)=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,(a-b),l.s.c.}(x)+H_{+}(x)\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)
for all
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. We need to show that
h
is the greatest lower semicontinuous function less or equal to
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
.

Suppose that
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
D(x)=\infty
. Then since
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,(a-b),l.s.c.}(x)=\infty
, we have
h(x)=\infty
. Suppose now that
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
D(x)<\infty
. For any sequence
(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
x_{n}\to x
as
n\to\infty
there exists
(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
such that
x_{n}=x+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}
,
\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, and
x+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Furthermore,
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\big(D(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})-D(x)\big)\geq 0
and thus
\liminf_{n\to\infty}D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\geq 0
. We show that
h
is lower semicontinuous by proving that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\big(h(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})-h(x)\big)=\liminf_{n\to\infty}\Big\{-\mu\big(D(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})-D(x)\big)+\frac{a}{2}\big(D(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})^{2}-D(x)^{2}\big)

(3.15)

\displaystyle-\frac{b}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}\big((x_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})^{2}-x_{k}^{2}\big)\Big)+\frac{1}{2(a-b)}\Big(\big(\mu-aD(x)\big)_{+}^{2}-\big(\mu-aD(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\big)_{+}^{2}\Big)\Big\}\geq 0.

We write
\varepsilon_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}=\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}
with
\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k},\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}\geq 0
. Clearly,
\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}\leq x_{k}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
We shall show that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}=0,

(3.16)

and that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}(x_{k}+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k})^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}+\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\big)^{2}.

(3.17)

As
D(x)<\infty
, there exists
C>0
such that
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{2}x_{k}<C
, and thus (
3.16
) follows as

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}\leq C\limsup_{n\to\infty}\lVert\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}\rVert_{\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}=0.

To obtain (
3.17
) we just expand

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}(x_{k}+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k})^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}+2\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}(x_{k}-\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k})\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}+\big(D(\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\big)^{2}.

The middle term vanishes due to (
3.16
). To show that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(D(\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\big)^{2}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\big)^{2}

note that
D(x)<\infty
implies that

D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})-D(\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})=D(\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\leq D(x)<\infty.

Hence, for any
\delta
there exists
K\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sum_{k=K+1}^{\infty}k\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}\leq\sum_{k=K+1}^{\infty}kx_{k}<\frac{\delta}{2}.

On the other hand, there exists for this
\delta
a
n(K)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sum_{k=1}^{K}k\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}<\frac{\delta}{2},\quad\mbox{ for all }n\geq n(K),

thus showing (
3.17
). We continue with

\mbox{ r.h.s. of \eqref{hlsc} }\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\Big\{\frac{1}{2(a-b)}\Big(\big(\mu-aD(x)\big)_{+}^{2}-\big(\mu-aD(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\big)_{+}^{2}\Big)-\big(\mu-aD(x)\big)D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\big)\\
\qquad+\frac{a-b}{2}\big(D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\big)^{2}\Big\}.

(3.18)

Recall that
\liminf_{n\to\infty}D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\geq 0
, and thus we know that
(\mu-aD(x))<0
implies that eventually
(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}))<0
and
(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})+bD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}))<0
.
Suppose that
\mu/a<D(x)
. Then

\mbox{r.h.s. of \eqref{hlsc2} }=\liminf_{n\to\infty}-\big(\mu-aD(x)\big)D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})+\frac{a-b}{2}D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})^{2}\geq 0.

Suppose
\mu/a\geq D(x)
and
\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\leq 0
. Then

\mbox{ r.h.s. of \eqref{hlsc2} }\geq\frac{1}{2(a-b)}\liminf_{n\to\infty}\Big(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})+bD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\Big)^{2}\geq 0,

and likewise for
\mu/a\geq D(x)
and
\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})>0
,

\displaystyle\mbox{r.h.s. of \eqref{hlsc2} }

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2(a-b)}\liminf_{n\to\infty}\Big\{\Big(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})+bD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle-\Big(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\Big)^{2}\Big\}\geq 0.

We have established (
3.15
) and thus the lower semicontinuity of
h
. We finally show that
h
is the largest lower semicontinuous function less or equal to
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
. Using the lower semicontinuity of
h
and
h\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
, we know that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\geq h(x)

for any sequence
x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}
with
x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}\to x
as
n\to\infty
. We pick now a particular sequence
x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}=x+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}
with

\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}={\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{k=n\}\frac{\big(\mu-aD(x)\big)_{+}}{n(a-b)}

(3.19)

to find that

h(x)\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}(x)-\frac{\big(\mu-aD(x)\big)_{+}}{2(a-b)}.

∎

Proposition 3.14

(
Upper bound HYL-model
)
.

For all
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\alpha\leq 0
, and
a>b\geq 0
,

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\Big]\leq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\Big\{I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(x)\Big\}.

(3.20)

Proof.
The statement follows easily with the upper bound estimate in Varadhan’s Lemma given in
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.6]
using the inequality
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha}(x)\geq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(x)\geq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,(a-b),l.s.c.}(x)
for all
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, the lower semicontinuity of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}
, and the fact that
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(x)\geq-\frac{\left(\mu+\alpha\right)^{2}}{2(a-b)}
. The later estimate provides the tail-condition necessary to apply
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.6]
.
∎

For the lower bound we are using the lower bound (
3.8
) for the PMF model and
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a}+H_{-}
with
H_{-}
being upper semicontinuous.

Proposition 3.15

(
Lower bound HYL-model
)
.

For all
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\alpha\leq 0
, and
a>b\geq 0
,

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\Big]\geq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\Big\{I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(x)\Big\}.

(3.21)

Proof.
Using

\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\Big]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\mu,\alpha}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H_{-}}\Big]Z^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}(\beta,\mu,\alpha)

in conjunction with the LDP in Theorem
1.4
and in particular the lower bound (
3.8
) we arrive at

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\mu,\alpha}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H_{-}}\Big]Z^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}(\beta,\mu,\alpha)\Big)\geq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\alpha}(x)+\beta H_{-}(x)\big\}-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\alpha}(x)\big\}

\displaystyle=-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,a,l.s.c.}(x)+\beta H_{-}(x)\big\}

\displaystyle=-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu+\alpha,l.s.c.}(x)\big\},

where the last equality follows from Lemma
3.16
below.

∎

Lemma 3.16
.

\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a,l.s.c.}(x)+\beta H_{-}(x)\big\}=\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}(x)\big\}

Proof.
The infimum of any function on an open set is equal to the infimum of its lower semicontinuous regularisation over the same set. Recall that
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
is open and that
I_{0}\equiv\infty
in
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})\setminus\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.
We thus need to show that

\big(H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a,l.s.c.}+H_{-}\big)_{l.s.c.}(x)=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}(x)\quad\mbox{ for all }x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}).

(3.22)

Note that

H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a}(x)+H_{-}(x)\geq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a,l.s.c.}(x)+H_{-}(x)\geq\big(H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a,l.s.c.}+H_{-}\big)_{l.s.c.}(x).

To show (
3.22
) we use the proof of Lemma
3.13
and choose the sequence according to (
3.19
). We thus obtain

H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}(x)\geq\big(H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a,l.s.c.}+H_{-}\big)_{l.s.c.}(x).

∎

We finally combine Proposition
3.14
and Proposition
3.15
to finish the proof for Theorem
1.4
.∎

3.5.
Proof of Theorem
1.9

The prove the first part of Theorem
1.9
requires first the limiting logarithmic moment generating function in Proposition
1.8
and an application of the Gärtner-Ellis theorem. In fact, the reference measure is an independent superposition of Poisson point processes but not identical distributed.

Proof of Proposition
1.8
.
For
t\in\mathbb{R}
we get, using the independence of the Poisson point processes with expectation
{\tt E}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\operatorname{bc}}}})}}_{k}
,

\displaystyle{\tt E}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\operatorname{bc}}}})}}\Big[{\rm e}^{t\sum_{k+1}^{\infty}k{\mathcal{N}}_{k}}\Big]=\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}{\tt E}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\operatorname{bc}}}})}}_{k}\Big[{\rm e}^{t\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k{\mathcal{N}}_{k}}\Big]=\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}{\rm e}^{tkm}\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{N}}_{k}=m)=\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}{\rm e}^{\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert q_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}({\rm e}^{tk}-1)},

and thus

\Lambda(t)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log{\tt E}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\operatorname{bc}}}})}}\Big[{\rm e}^{t\sum_{k+1}^{\infty}k{\mathcal{N}}_{k}}\Big]=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}\big({\rm e}^{tk}-1\big).

∎

Proof of Theorem
1.9
.
(a) This is a straightforward application of the Gärtner-Ellis theorem, see
[
DZ09
]
. To ensure that
0
is in the domain of the logarithmic moment generating function we need to have
\alpha<0
. Then the rate function is giving as the Legendre-Fenchel transform

\displaystyle J_{\alpha}(x)

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big\{tx-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}\big({\rm e}^{tk}-1\big)\Big\}=\beta\Big(p(\beta,\alpha)+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big\{x\frac{t}{\beta}-p(\beta,\alpha+\frac{t}{\beta})\big\}\Big)

\displaystyle=\beta\Big(p(\beta,\alpha)+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big\{(\frac{t}{\beta}+\alpha)x-p(\beta,\alpha+\frac{t}{\beta})\big\}-x\alpha\Big)

\displaystyle=\beta\Big(p(\beta,\alpha)+f(\beta,x)-\alpha x\Big),

where we used that for
x\leq\varrho_{\rm c}
,

f(\beta,x)=\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\{\alpha x-p(\beta,\alpha)\}.

Clearly,
J_{\alpha}(x)=\infty
when
x<0
as the empirical density only takes positive values. Suppose that
x>\varrho_{\rm c}
fo dimensions
d\geq 3
(
\varrho_{\rm c}=\infty
for
d=1,2
). Then

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big\{(\frac{t}{\beta}+\alpha)\varrho_{\rm c}+(\frac{t}{\beta}+\alpha)(x-\varrho_{\rm c})-p(\beta,\alpha+\frac{t}{\beta})\big\}\geq f(\beta,\varrho_{\rm c})+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big\{(\frac{t}{\beta}+\alpha)(x-\varrho_{\rm c})\big\}=+\infty,

and thus
J_{\alpha}(x)
for
x\notin[0,\varrho_{\rm c}]
.

(b) This is straightforward application of Varadhan’s Lemma in
[
DZ09
, Theorem 4.3.1]
using that
h(x):=x\mapsto-\mu x+\frac{a}{2}x^{2}
is continuous and the fact that the tail-condition is satisfied

\lim_{M\to\infty}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log{\tt E}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert h(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{n})}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{h(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{n})\geq M\}\Big]=-\infty.

∎

4.
Proofs: variational analysis and Bose-Einstein condensation (BEC)

4.1.
Zeroes of the rate functions

We collect all proofs for the zeroes o four rate functions.

Proof of Proposition
2.1
.
To find the zeroes of the ideal gas rate function, first let us find the critical points by setting the Gâteaux derivative of the function to zero. That is, we find the set of points
\tilde{x}\in\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)
such that

{\rm d}I_{\mu}\left(\tilde{x};y\right)=0\quad\forall y\in\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}\right).

This yields a single equation for each element of the sequence
\tilde{x}
. This set of equations has the unique solution
\tilde{x}=\xi
given by

\xi_{k}=q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mu}})}}_{k},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}.

(4.1)

Since the rate function
I_{\mu}
is strictly convex where it is finite, this critical point is the unique global minimiser.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.2
.
For the existence of a minimiser, recall that
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}}_{\mu}
is lower semicontinuous and has compact level-sets. Also note that
I_{\mu}
is strictly convex where it is finite and
H^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}}
is convex. Therefore
I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
is strictly convex where it is finite (a non-empty set) and uniqueness of the minimiser follows.

To calculate the minimiser, we search for stationary points. Since
I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}
is strictly convex where it is finite, if we find a stationary point then it is the global minimiser. By considering the coordinate derivatives, we know that the minimiser must satisfy all the following equations:

\log\frac{x_{k}}{q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mu}})}}_{k}}+a\beta\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}x_{k}=0,\quad k\in\mathbb{N}.

To make this more manageable, we introduce the dummy variable
\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and corresponding equation
\Gamma=\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}x_{k}
. Our problem is then to solve

\displaystyle\log\frac{x_{k}}{q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mu}})}}_{k}}+a\beta\Gamma

\displaystyle=0,\quad k\in\mathbb{N},

(4.2)

\displaystyle\Gamma-\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}x_{k}

\displaystyle=0.

(4.3)

Given
\Gamma
, (
4.2
) is uniquely solved by
x_{k}=q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mu}})}}_{k}\exp\left(-a\beta\Gamma\right),k\in\mathbb{N}
, and therefore (
4.3
) becomes

\Gamma=\exp\left(-a\beta\Gamma\right)\bar{q}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mu}})}}.

This has the unique solution
\Gamma=\frac{1}{a\beta}W_{0}\left(a\beta\bar{q}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mu}})}}\right)=\frac{1}{a\beta}W_{0}\left(K\right)
,
and so (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) are uniquely jointly solved by
x=\xi
given by

\xi_{k}=q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mu}})}}_{k}\exp\left(-W_{0}\left(K\right)\right)=\frac{W_{0}\left(K\right)}{K}q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mu}})}}_{k},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
2.4
.
Without loss of generality we put
\alpha\equiv 0
, and write
q_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({0}})}}=q_{k},k\in\mathbb{N}
and
I_{0}=I
. To obtain the unique zero of the rate function we shall find the unique minimiser of the un-normalised rate function
F(x):=I(x)+\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}(x)
. For the existence of a minimiser, recall that
F
is lower semicontinuous and has compact level-sets. Also note that
I
is strictly convex where it is finite, and
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,l.s.c.}
is also convex in the linear function
D(x)
. Therefore
F
is strictly convex where it is finite ( a non-empty set) and uniqueness of the minimiser follows. To calculate the minimiser, we search for stationary points. Since
F
is strictly convex where it is finite, if we find a stationary point then it is the global minimiser. By considering again as in the proof of Proposition
2.1
the coordinate derivatives, we know that the minimiser must satisfy all the following equations

\log\frac{x_{k}}{q_{k}}+\beta k\big(aD(x)-\mu\big)_{+}=0,\quad k\in\mathbb{N}.

To make this more manageable, we introduce the dummy variable
\delta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and corresponding equation
\delta=D(x)
.

\displaystyle\log\frac{x_{k}}{q_{k}}+\beta k\big(a\delta-\mu\big)_{+}

\displaystyle=0,\quad k\in\mathbb{N},

(4.4)

\displaystyle\delta-D(x)

\displaystyle=0.

(4.5)

Given the value
\delta
, (
4.4
) is uniquely solved by
x_{k}=q_{k}\exp\big(\beta k\big(\mu-a\delta\big)_{-}\big),k\in\mathbb{N},
and therefore (
4.5
) becomes

\delta=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kq_{k}\exp\big(\beta k\big(\mu-a\delta\big)_{-}\big).

(4.6)

Denote the right hand side in (
4.6
) by
h(\delta)
, and note that
h(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to\infty
. Furthermore,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}h(\delta)=\begin{cases}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}kq_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mu}})}}\in(0,\infty)&,\mu<0,\\
\infty&,d=1,2\mbox{ and }\mu\geq 0,\\
\varrho_{\rm c}\in(0,\infty)&,d\geq 3\mbox{ and }\mu\geq 0,\end{cases}

see Figure
5
. In all cases there exists a unique solution which we denote
\delta^{*}
.

0
h\left(\delta\right)
\delta
\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}

(a)

d=1,2

0
h\left(\delta\right)
\delta
\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}
\varrho_{\mathrm{c}}

(b)

d\geq 3

Figure 5
.
Sketch of
h\left(\delta\right)
. This shows
\mu+\alpha>0
, but the sketch translates with
\mu
.

∎

Proof of Proposition
2.6
.
Without loss of generality we put
\alpha\equiv 0
, and write
q_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({0}})}}=q_{k},k\in\mathbb{N}
, and write
I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,0}=I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
. For the existence of such a minimiser
\xi
, recall that
I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\alpha}
is lower semicontinuous and has compact level-sets.

Suppose that the partial derivative
\frac{\partial I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}{\partial x_{k}}
is defined and non-zero at
x\in\mathrm{Int}\left\{\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)\right\}
. Then
I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
does not achieve its infimum at
x
. Since the boundary
\partial\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)=\left\{x\in\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right):\exists\,k\text{ s.t. }x_{k}=0\right\}
, and
\frac{\partial I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}{\partial x_{k}}=-\infty
here, the infimum is not achieved here.

For
x\in\mathrm{Int}\left\{\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)\right\}
we have

\frac{\partial I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}{\partial x_{k}}(x)=\log\frac{x_{k}}{q_{k}}-b\beta k^{2}x_{k}-\beta k\left(\mu-aD\left(x\right)\right)\begin{Bmatrix}1&,aD\left(x\right)\geq\mu\\
-\frac{b}{a-b}&,aD\left(x\right)\leq\mu\end{Bmatrix},\qquad k\in\mathbb{N},

which is defined everywhere in
\mathrm{Int}\left\{\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)\right\}
. Hence a solution
\xi
must solve
\frac{\partial I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}{\partial x_{k}}(\xi)=0
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. To make this more manageable, we introduce the dummy variable
\delta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and corresponding equation
\delta=D\left(x\right)
. Our problem is then to solve

\displaystyle\log\frac{x_{k}}{q_{k}}-b\beta k^{2}x_{k}-\beta k\left(\mu-a\delta\right)\begin{Bmatrix}1&,a\delta\geq\mu\\
-\frac{b}{a-b}&,a\delta\leq\mu\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad k\in\mathbb{N},

(4.7)

\displaystyle\delta-D\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=0.

(4.8)

Unfortunately - unlike in the corresponding PMF case - even when we are given
\delta
we are not guaranteed to have a solution for (
4.7
), or that such a solution would be unique. If we fix
\delta
, then the
k^{th}
equation of (
4.7
) either has no solution or is solved by

x_{k}=-\frac{1}{b\beta k^{2}}W_{\chi_{k}}\Big(-b\beta k^{2}q_{k}\exp\Big[\beta k\Big(\mu-a\delta\Big)\begin{Bmatrix}1&,a\delta\geq\mu\\
-\frac{b}{a-b}&,a\delta\leq\mu\end{Bmatrix}\Big]\Big)

for all
\chi_{k}\in\left\{0,-1\right\}
, where
W_{0}
and
W_{-1}
are the two real branches of the Lambert W function. The ‘no solution’ case corresponds precisely to
W_{0}
and
W_{-1}
not being defined for this input.

Substituting these
x_{k}
back into (
4.8
) gives the condition (
2.5
) as required.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.7
.
The essence of this proof is to find a set of parameters for which Proposition
2.6
produces only one candidate zero. First note that where they are finite,
g^{\chi}
are continuous. Also, for
\chi\neq 0
we have
g^{\chi}\left(\delta\right)\sim C_{\chi}\delta
as
\delta\to+\infty
where
C\chi\geq\frac{a}{b}>1
. Because
g^{\chi}
has a translational symmetry with
\mu+\alpha
, this means that for sufficiently negative
\mu+\alpha
these branches will have no solution. In contrast,
g^{0}
is continuous and decreasing for
\delta>\frac{\mu+\alpha}{a}
. This means that as we decrease
\mu+\alpha
we are eventually guaranteed to have a unique solution for
\delta
.
∎

4.2.
Proofs: pressure representations, thermodynamic behaviour and BEC

Theorem
2.8
allows to analyse the thermodynamic limit. Note that the partial derivative of the pressure with respect to the chemical potential is the expected physical particle density. This particle density depends on the thermodynamic systems, and its value can equal the density of the minimisers or it can exceed the density of the minimisers. In the latter case one experiences an excess density which is the particle mass condensing in the so-called BEC state.
The following result will be useful in calculating the derivatives of these pressures.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be an open interval,
F:\ell_{1}\times I\to\mathbb{R}
, and
\xi\in C^{1}\left(I;\ell_{1}\right)
. Also define

\mathcal{G}:I\to\mathbb{R};s\mapsto F\left(\xi\left(s\right),s\right).

Then if
F\left(x,s\right)
is Gâteaux differentiable in its first argument at
\xi\left(s\right)
with
\left.\frac{\partial F}{\partial x_{k}}\right|_{\xi}=0

\forall k\in\mathbb{N}
, then

\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{G}}{{\rm d}s}=\left.\frac{\partial F}{\partial s}\right|_{\xi}.

Proof.
An application of the chain rule gives

\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{G}}{{\rm d}s}=\left.\frac{\partial F}{\partial s}\right|_{\xi}+\sum^{\infty}_{j=1}\frac{{\rm d}\xi_{k}}{{\rm d}s}\left.\frac{\partial F}{\partial x_{k}}\right|_{x=\xi}.

Since the partial derivatives of
F
with respect to
x_{k}
vanish at
\xi
, we only keep the first term.
∎

Ideal Bose gas.

Proof of Proposition
2.9
.
(1) Clearly,
p(\beta,0)=\frac{1}{\beta(4\pi\beta)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k^{1+d/2}}<\infty
for all
d\geq 1
